C O R P O R AT E OV E RV I E W

The Next 25
Building a secure and self-reliant energy future for India
As electricity demand continues
to rise, power grids are becoming
the backbone of India’s power
sector, enabling transmission of
electricity from generation hubs to
consumption centers across India’s
vast, unified “One Nation, One
Grid, One Frequency” network.
This is critical for balancing load,
integrating renewable energy and
facilitating economic growth and
social development.
The Next 25 is the Company's
guiding principle for accelerating
grid infrastructure with pioneered
and proven technologies and
systems which will power
humanity’s next 25 years of
progress. With scale, technology
and people, transforming the
energy system and powering the
next industrial revolution, the AI age

01

and the transition to clean energy,
for today and for generations
to come.
Powered by innovative solutions,
advanced grid technologies and
integrated solutions, the Company
supports India in synchronizing the
grid, integrating renewable energy
and digitalizing the infrastructure for
cleaner, sustainable energy access.
With unmatched operational
excellence and tech expertise, we
support customers in their digital
and AI transformation journeys. The
Company continues to drive the
transition to a fully electrified world,
supported by an energy system that
is secure, accessible, affordable
and sustainable. This enables
societies to prosper today while
building a stronger foundation for
future generations.

Moving in step with
India’s energy security
agenda and carbon
neutral journey, The
Company is enabling a
cleaner, more flexible
and secure power grid.
With this, your Company
is building a resilient
nation that meets the
growing demand for
electricity – affordably,
reliably and sustainably
and makes it available
across long distances.

About the report
This Integrated Annual Report of Hitachi Energy India Limited provides a concise
and thorough evaluation of how the Company's long-term vision has continued to
drive value creation for stakeholders. The Report details the Company’s strategy for
creating value across economic and non-economic areas, aligned with a commitment
to transparency.
Adherence to reporting guidelines
The financial and statutory data
presented in the Report is in
accordance with international
frameworks. These include
reporting requirements under the
Companies Act, 2013 and the
rules made thereunder, Indian
Accounting Standards (IndAS), the
Securities and Exchange Board of
India (SEBI), Listing Obligations and
Disclosure Requirements (LODR)
Regulations, 2015, in accordance
with the core requirement of the
Global Reporting Initiative (GRI)
Standards 2021, the National
Guidelines on Responsible
Business Conduct (NGRBC),
Business Responsibility and
Sustainability Reporting (mandated
by SEBI) and the UN Sustainable
Development Goals (UN SDGs),
among others. The non-financial
data in the Report is guided by the
International Integrated Reporting
Council (IIRC) framework.

emphasizes what matters most to
the stakeholders and the business
and explains how it identifies and
addresses material issues.
To provide business context, the
Company outlines the strategic
pillars and explains how they
influence its business presence and
business units. With a well-defined
business model, the Company
illustrates how its long-term plan
has consistently created value for
the stakeholders, while ensuring
sustainable organizational growth.
The Company wants to mention
that there are no restatements
or corrections to the information
provided in the Report from the
previous reporting year.
Reporting period
The Company’s Integrated Annual
Report is produced and published
annually. This Report provides
information for the financial year
April 1, 2025 to March 31, 2026.

Scope of reporting

Reporting boundary

This Integrated Annual Report
has been prepared with reference
to the International Integrated
Reporting Council’s (IIRC)
International Integrated Reporting
Framework. The Company
understands that the Integrated
Annual Report framework provides
a useful basis for disclosing how
it creates sustainable value for its
stakeholders. The Report aims
to present a holistic review of
how the Company performed
in FY 2025-26. Your Company

The information presented in
the Report is material to the
stakeholders and presents an
overview of the businesses and
associated activities that help in
creating value in the short, medium
and long term. The information
presented in the Integrated Annual
Report covers information on
quantitative as well as qualitative
data on environmental, social
and governance parameters
encompassing all the operational
locations within India. The

boundary and coverage considered
for financial disclosures and
non-financial disclosures is the
same and there are no other
entities considered.
Board responsibility statement

been read and reviewed by the

under the guidance of the
Board. This ensures the integrity,
accuracy and completeness of the
information disclosed in the Report.
Source of data

economic data contained in this
Annual Report has been obtained

party reports which are believed
to be reliable. While the Company
believes such information to be
accurate, it has not independently
verified such data and makes
no representation as to its
completeness or accuracy.

factors referred above are not all-inclusive and are in addition to other factors contained elsewhere in this report and in
other materials published by the Company.

About Hitachi Energy India Limited

Powering a
cleaner energy
future for India
Aimed at societal progress
Hitachi Energy India Limited (referred to as
“Hitachi Energy”), the Indian subsidiary of the
Zurich-headquartered Hitachi Energy Ltd, which
is a global leader in electrification, powering the
electricity era to meet the energy demands of
today and the next 25 years.
Hitachi Energy Ltd is a part of Hitachi Group,
a global leader in digital innovation and
sustainable solutions and delivers clean energy
and power grid systems across the world,
ensuring a smart, sustainable and better future
for all.
With over a century in pioneering mission-critical
technologies like high-voltage, transformers,
automation and power electronics, Hitachi
Energy Ltd continues to address the most urgent
energy challenge of balancing soaring electricity
demand, while decarbonizing the power system.
Carrying forward Hitachi Energy Ltd’s 115-yearold purpose into the future
Hitachi Energy India Limited is committed to
driving India’s energy transition ambitions. Your
Company is on a mission to deliver its purpose
– Inspire the next era of sustainable energy.
Your Company’s technological innovations
are supporting initiatives towards ensuring
reliable, affordable, cleaner and more secure
energy for every household and enterprise
and the seamless integration of renewables
at scale across multiple projects, while
adopting digitalization.

C O R P O R AT E OV E RV I E W

A reliable and resilient technology leader
Hitachi Energy is a trusted technology partner
for energy infrastructure. With innovative
technologies and services, the Company helps
make the energy value chain more efficient,
making electricity more accessible to all. The
pioneering solutions of your Company span
across the entire value chain making it a holistic
lifecycle partner, while also providing reliable
and sustainable services. As a pioneering
technology leader, the Company collaborates
with customers and partners to inspire the next
era of sustainable energy.
Value proposition
Your Company offers innovative solutions and
services in the utility, renewables, industry,
transport and infrastructure sectors. Your
Company pioneers innovative technologies by
collaborating with its customers and partners,
continuing its endeavor towards the energy
transition movement. With a diverse portfolio

05

of smart solutions (products and services),
your Company aims to create a sustainable and
cleaner energy ecosystem.
Your Company’s comprehensive energy portfolio
offers sustainable solutions to meet the growing
demand for electricity. With its wide range of
transmission and distribution solutions, your
Company is perfectly aligned with the growing
requirement for efficient power evacuation
from renewable energy sources to make the
grid flexible and reliable and to drive India’s
energy transition.
Some of these solutions include transformers,
high voltage, power quality, High Voltage Direct
Current (HVDC), grid integration, automation
solutions and Flexible AC Transmission Systems
(FACTS).

© 2026 Hitachi Energy. All rights reserved.

Key partnerships
Your Company engages in meaningful partnerships across sectors including utility, renewables, industry, transport
and infrastructure, with a key focus on building truly sustainable power systems. These diverse partnerships are
aimed at creating innovative pathways for the customers and other partners with an objective of making today’s
power systems strong enough to support a carbon-neutral future.

Integrated Annual Report 2025-26

C O R P O R AT E OV E RV I E W

Dear Shareholders,
The world’s energy demand is
growing rapidly amid a complex
transition that seeks to balance
energy security with sustainability.
This underscores the need for
decarbonization and the growing
importance of an efficient, reliable,
secure and clean energy system.
As nations strive to meet the
increasing demand, secure
energy supply and reduce carbon
emissions, power grids play a
critical role in this transition. Energy
investments are accelerating
across sectors, including utilities,
industries, transportation and
infrastructure. But this is not
enough. By 2040, over 80 million
kilometers of grid infrastructure will
need upgrades, requiring estimated
annual investments of USD 600
billion. Globally, more than 9,000
companies have joined the race
to Net Zero and pledged to take
rigorous, immediate action to halve
global emissions by 2030.
Despite this momentum towards
cleaner, sustainable and secure
energy, the current landscape
presents unique challenges too.
Shifting priorities and unpredictable
geopolitical environment are
affecting the pace of the energy
transition and slowing efforts
toward clean energy.
This has reignited global discussion
on energy security. In India, there
is a clear ask for an indigenized
supply chain that is insulated from
external disruptions.
In this fast-evolving energy
scenario, Hitachi Energy India
Ltd. is uniquely positioned as an
enabler of a resilient and secure
energy system.
Source:
UN
-

India’s journey towards clean
energy transition
India continues to adopt innovative
strategies to build a cleaner energy
system. It has taken key measures

13

like increasing the share and uptake
of installed renewable capacity in
all spheres of life.
During the year, India recorded a
landmark in its energy transition
journey, surpassing its COP-26
goals by successfully building its
non-fossil fuel installed base to
50% of its electricity generation
capacity. The milestone includes
reaching over 500 GW of total
installed capacity, driven by
massive increases in solar and
wind contributions, underscoring
India’s steadfast commitment to
climate action and sustainable
development. It was achieved more
than five years ahead of its 2030
target set under its Nationally
Determined Contributions (NDC) to
the Paris Agreement. The notable
achievement comes with the
challenge of seamlessly integrating
intermittent energy into the
national grid.
India is expected to witness energy
sector investments of around USD
170 billion in 2026, according
to the International Energy
Agency (IEA), led by renewables
and transmission infrastructure
expansion. The nation aims to
achieve net-zero emissions by
2070, with the revised goal of
900 GW of installed non-fossil
capacity by 2035. Additionally,
the government is shifting its
focus from capacity expansion to
capacity absorption, emphasizing
grid integration, energy storage,
hybridization and market reforms to
meet national energy goals. In this
context, digital transformation is
key and there is a growing demand
for transformative technologies to
transition to clean energy.
Partnering India in the energy
transition journey
Hitachi Energy is a global leader
in electrification, powering the
electricity era to meet today's
and tomorrow's energy demands.
The Company is advancing the
nation’s energy transition through

sustainable, digital solutions, with
a strong focus on renewables, data
centers and railway electrification.
With deep expertise in renewable
technologies, robust local
manufacturing capabilities and
a strong portfolio of innovative
products and solutions, we are
helping to strengthen India’s
energy infrastructure and play a
key role in India’s progress.
We are driving the next phase of
growth by supporting:

– HVDC transmission.
– Seamless integration
of renewable.

– Smart, digital grids and
lifecycle services.

– Electrified mobility solutions
– AI-ready data centers.
We have been delivering key
power projects in India with
sustainability at the heart of
everything we do. This reflects our
commitment to a clean, secure
and sustainable energy future.
Given our expertise in renewable
energy technologies and our local
manufacturing capabilities, we are
proud to be engaged in several
nation-building projects.
Capitalizing technology leadership
As one of the leading power
tech companies, we are driving
India’s electrification and grid
modernization and are proud to
support India’s growing grid and
renewable energy integration while
strengthening the local energy
manufacturing ecosystem for a
more sustainable future.
Hitachi Energy India Limited’s key
priority is sustainability and we
remain aligned with the nation
in its sustainability journey. Our
emphasis remains on integrating
remote renewable energy into the
national grid. Our strong order
inflows and export momentum are
reinforcing market leadership and
long-term growth visibility.

© 2026 Hitachi Energy. All rights reserved.

We have been delivering key power
projects in India with sustainability
at the heart of everything we do.
This reflects our commitment to
a clean, flexible and sustainable
energy future. Given our expertise
in renewable energy technologies
and our local manufacturing
capabilities, we are proud to be
engaged in several nation-building
projects. Implemented across
diverse projects and locations,
our sustainability initiatives aim
to reduce environmental impact
while staying aligned with Hitachi
Energy’s Global 2030 strategic
growth objectives. At the same
time, we are firmly committed
to inspiring the next era of
sustainable energy.
Furthermore, we are proud to be
at the forefront of high-growth
areas, powering AI-ready data
centers and the sustainable energy
future ahead. We are leveraging
digitalization to make India’s energy
system more flexible and secure
and to contribute to a better future.
Consistent performance
Our performance during
the financial year 2025-26
highlights the increasing pace
of electrification in India and
the world, with global electricity
demand projected to surge and
with Asia accounting for over 70%
of this growth.
Our focus on advanced grid
technologies, digitalization and
integrated solutions is reflected
in our operational and financial
performance. A continued focus
on effective order execution and
overall operational efficiency
helped the Company sustain its
growth momentum. The Company
delivered a robust performance,
supported by sustained operational
excellence and growth momentum,
strong order execution, a good
product mix, increased export
momentum and a robust order
backlog that reinforces its
market leadership.

Integrated Annual Report 2025-26

Your Company continued its strong
revenue growth in FY 2025-26,
with YoY growth of 27.60%,
reaching ` 8,147.70 Crores.
This is largely due to the robust
demand in India and other key
markets, expanded market share
and successful execution of
strategic initiatives.
PAT increased 157% to ` 987.80
Crores, driven by higher revenues
from core operations, efficient
execution and continued focus
on cost management. A healthy
operating EBITDA margin of
15.40% reinforced our operational
strength and strategic agility. The
growing order book was led by
orders for transformers (power,
traction and dry) reactors, gasinsulated switchgear (GIS), airinsulated switchgear (AIS) and
locomotive transformers.
The order book increased, with
the highest-ever order backlog of
` 29,555.30 Crores (up 53.50%
YoY), reflecting strong operational
momentum and providing revenue
visibility for several upcoming
quarters and market leadership.
Your Company received export
orders from the US, Europe
and APAC.
Growth drivers among business
segments
In terms of business segments,
industries and renewables were
the key contributors to the order
book, followed by transmission and
transport. To enhance customer
experience, your Company
launched Services as its 5th
business unit under a strengthened,
future-focused global service
framework. Some of the key service
orders came from state utilities,
data centers, transmission and
industry for system refurbishment,
SCADA upgrades and life
cycle services.
The Services unit is key to
addressing the challenges of
upgrading grid infrastructure

by extending asset lifecycles,
improving efficiency and investing
in service solutions enabled by
digital technologies. Through a
cradle-to-renewal approach, your
Company’s comprehensive service
portfolio supports customers at
every stage of their asset lifecycle
– from planning through installation
and commissioning to lifetime
extension and sustainable end-oflife solutions.
The Board approved an investment
of ` 2,000 Crores to set up, in
addition to others, a greenfield
large power transformers facility
in Karjan, Vadodara, Gujarat. This
investment is in addition to the
capital expenditure commitment
the Company announced in
October 2024, taking the
cumulative capex commitment to
` 4,000 Crores.
Outlook
India’s power demand reached a
new record of 244 GW in early
CY 2026, driven by industrial
activity and varying winter
temperatures, with projections
indicating a further surge in the
summer. This marks a significant
increase from the FY 2025-26
peak, which reached roughly 250
GW in May and August.
The nation significantly reduced
its energy shortages, transforming
from a power-deficient country to
one with universal electricity access
and power sufficiency. Key factors
contributing to this transition
included massive infrastructure
expansion, a rapid shift toward
renewable energy and financial
reforms in the distribution sector.
India is rapidly emerging as a
global energy leader and is the
world’s third-largest renewable
energy producer, driven by massive
solar growth, strong investment
and policy reforms. Its emphasis
remains on achieving its goals
for net zero and energy security.
This includes emerging sectors

14

C O R P O R AT E OV E RV I E W

like battery energy storage, green
hydrogen and advanced biofuels,
driven by policies such as the
National Green Hydrogen Mission
and well positioned to meet its
energy targets.
The Middle East conflict disrupted
global oil supply and led to a
widespread energy crisis. This
resulted in an oil price surge,
impacted economies and prompted
calls for renewable energy
investment. Although India has
also been affected by the West
Asia conflict it remains one of
the fastest-growing economies.
Opportunities continue to increase
as India moves toward its larger
energy goals. Strong domestic
demand, steady investments and
a resilient external sector drive
a positive outlook. Key factors
such as a favorable monsoon,
GST 2.0 reforms, easier access
to credit and rising capacity
utilization have contributed to the
growing momentum.
The recent EU India FTA
strengthens clean energy
collaboration, especially in
renewables and green hydrogen
and is likely to boost opportunities
for Indian energy firms through
enhanced technology exchange
and investment flows. It is also
likely to create a more stable,
climate-aligned trade framework,
helping Indian energy players
expand their exports and attract
long-term capital for infrastructure
and innovation.
However, the challenge ahead is
not only to supply electricity, but to
manage it under more demanding
conditions. Capacity, control
and coordination will determine
whether the power system
can keep pace with industrial
complexity and urban growth.
To achieve this, a collaborative,
structured approach is required
from all stakeholders, supported
by strong local manufacturing and
technology capabilities.

Operating environment

Indian economy – marked by growth, stability and confidence
Year 2025-26 marked an inflection
point. Policy overhauls across
Western economies – particularly
in trade, investment and industrial
policy – triggered spillover effects
across all major global market
and India was not immune to
these shifts.
India and the European Union
concluded negotiations for
a comprehensive Free Trade
Agreement (India-EU FTA) at the
16th EU Summit, marking a historic

Integrated Annual Report 2025-26

milestone in India-EU economic
relations and trade engagement
with key global partners. The
FTA offers massive benefits to
India’s labor-intensive sectors as
the deal covers goods, services
and investment protection, with
a number of India’s tariff lines
covered. The agreement, aimed
at deepening economic ties and
fostering market access, positions
India and EU as trusted partners
committed to open markets,
predictability and inclusive growth.

Towards the year-end, the nation
faced external shocks and acute
effects from global policy changes,
including tariff escalations and
volatile capital flows.
The government rationalized the
GST slabs ahead of the festive
season to boost domestic demand
(offsetting low export demand),
aid informal sector recovery,
strengthen tax compliance and
increase consumer spending.

16

C O R P O R AT E OV E RV I E W

Strengthening growth momentum1
Despite the challenging global
environment, India stood out as
the economy remained resilient
with robust growth, historically low
inflation, improving labor market
indicators and strengthening
external and financial buffers.
FY 2025-26 witnessed strong
economic momentum supported
by stable macroeconomic
fundamentals, sustained policy
support and broad-based
sectoral performance. India’s real
GDP expected to grow 7.60%
in FY 2025-26, up from 7.10%
in FY 2024-25, led by resilient
domestic demand amidst global
trade and policy uncertainties. Real
gross value added (GVA) expanded
by 7.70%, catalyzed by buoyant
industrial and services sectors.
The nation focused squarely on
its biggest strength of domestic
demand to keep growth buoyant
as inflation levels stayed low
at 1.80% on average through
the fiscal year. Sustained
international confidence along
with robust domestic demand,
falling unemployment and easing
inflation, position the country
well to advance steadily towards
its 2047 development goals.
India remained the fastest-growing
major economy with strong
macroeconomic fundamentals
and sustained growth momentum.
The growth momentum in the
economy reflects India’s resilience
amid persistent global trade
uncertainties. Domestic drivers-led
by robust private consumption
played a central role in supporting
this expansion.
India is among the world’s
fastest-growing major
economies and is well-positioned
to sustain the momentum. With

the ambition of attaining high
middle-income status by 2047- the
centenary year of its independence
– the country is building on
strong foundations of economic
growth, structural reforms and
social progress.
Changing fiscal policies2
Coordinated fiscal, monetary
and structural policies have
reinforced macroeconomic stability,
while supporting investment,
consumption and inclusion. With
slowing global demand, rising trade
frictions and a delicate domestic
consumption environment, India
deployed a carefully sequenced
set of fiscal, monetary and trade
reforms that not only cushioned
the economy, but also laid the
foundation for future growth.
The government and the
Reserve Bank of India (RBI)
remained aligned in their
resolve to strengthen domestic
demand. While fiscal policy
focused on boosting consumer
spending through tax relief and
public investment, monetary
policy provided liquidity and
cost-of-capital support needed to
sustain the recovery.
As India experienced an
unexpected slowdown in credit
growth, RBI delivered one of the
sharpest easing cycles in recent
history, cutting policy rates by
a full percentage point within
four months starting in February.
With food inflation cooling
sharply and global energy prices
stable, the central bank used
this window to shift decisively
from prolonged tightening to an
accommodative stance.
On an upward growth trajectory3
Looking ahead, India’s domestic
growth is on an upward trajectory
owing to multiple factors such as

India remained the
fastest-growing major
economy with strong
macroeconomic
fundamentals and
sustained growth
momentum

robust domestic demand, income
tax and goods and services tax
(GST) rationalization, softer
crude oil prices, front-loading of
government capital expenditure,
along with facilitative monetary and
financial conditions, supported by
benign inflation.
In a major milestone, S&P Global
upgraded India’s sovereign rating in
August 2025 from BBB– to BBB,
with short-term rating upgraded
to ‘A-2’ from ‘A-3’, marking its
first such upgrade in 18 years.
Domestic drivers such as favorable
agricultural prospects, sustained
effects of GST rationalization,
benign inflation and strong
balance sheets of corporates and
financial institutions – coupled with
supportive monetary and financial
conditions, are expected to
continue bolstering the economic
activity. External factors such as
services exports are projected
to remain robust, while the swift
conclusion of current trade and
investment negotiations offers
additional upside potential.
As India enters FY 2026-27,
several themes are seen shaping
the next phase of growth and
demand the same level of
pragmatism. Reforms – including
the rollout of GST 2.0 and tax-relief
measures – are unlikely to derail
this trajectory. Higher non-tax
revenues, driven by an accelerated
disinvestment pipeline and strategic
asset monetization, are expected to

India’s growth outlook remains

Fastest growth in IIP and
manufacturing PMI5
The Index of Industrial
Production(IIP), which tracks
the volume of output across
manufacturing, mining and
electricity, recorded a growth of
4.10% in the month of March,
2026 as compared to the March,
2025. Growth was led by the
mining and manufacturing sectors,
which expanded by 5.50% and
4.30% respectively, while electricity
generation recorded modest

growth of 0.80%. Overall, the
IIP index increased to 173.20
in March 2026 from 166.30
in March 2025, reflecting
continued expansion in industrial
output across key sectors. The
momentum was also mirrored
in Manufacturing Purchasing
Managers’ Index (PMI) for
manufacturing. In June 2025,
PMI stood at 58.40, climbing
to 59.10 in July 2025, it’s highest
in 16 months, before inching up
further to 59.30 in August 2025,
signaling the fastest improvement
in operating conditions in over
17 years.
The uptick was driven by a
sharp acceleration in production
volumes-the quickest pace
in nearly five years alongside
robust growth in new factory
orders. Companies responded
by scaling up input purchases
and expanding their workforce,
underscoring confidence in the
sector’s near-term prospects.
Remarkable progress in
infrastructure sector
Investment in infrastructure has
consistently proven to be one
of the most powerful engines of
economic growth in India and
occupies a central position in
India’s long-term development
strategy and the vision of Viksit
Bharat by 2047.
Over the years, the nation has
made remarkable progress in
infrastructure development
over the past decade. The
total infrastructure investment
in India has significantly
increased, with public and
private sector contributions
shaping the growth trajectory.
Public capital expenditure is
proposed to be increased by
~9% from FY 2025-26 (Budget

Estimates) to ` 12.20 Lakh Crores
in 2026-27, reinforcing the role of
public investment as a key driver
of economic growth and instilling
strong confidence in India’s
growth trajectory, anchored in
manufacturing, infrastructure and
consumption.
India – 3rd largest consumer of
electricity6
India’s power sector is among
the most diversified in the world,
drawing from conventional sources
such as coal, lignite, natural gas, oil,
hydro and nuclear power, as well as
non-conventional sources including
wind, solar and biomass. Rapidly
rising electricity demand continues
to drive capacity expansion, making
large-scale additions to generation
capacity essential for the future.
India is the third-largest producer
and consumer of electricity in the
world, with an installed capacity of
532.74 GW as of March 2026. In
FY 2025-26, the country generated
1,845.92 BU of power, a 1.30%
YoY increase over FY 2024-25.
Growing demand for electricity7
Electricity consumption remains led
by industry at 41.80%, followed by
households at 24.30%, agriculture
at 17% and commercial use at
8.30%. Consumption growth
has been steady, with power use
rising 33% between FY 2021 and
FY 2025 at a five-year CAGR of
7.40%. The country’s peak power
demand crossed 250 GW mark
for the first time in May 2024.
The highest demand recorded
in FY 2026-27 was 242.77 GW
in June 2025. In FY 2026-27,
peak power demand touched an
all-time high of 270.82 GW as
on May 21, 2026, significantly
higher than 230.99 GW recorded
in May 2025. The record level
was achieved amid soaring
temperatures across several parts
of the country.

Integrating renewable energy into

India’s renewable energy capacity

to 274.68 GW (as on March, 2026)
in FY 2025-26.
Transmission system has been

of power from over 900 GW
non-fossil capacity by FY 2035-36.
The associated transmission
schemes are at various stages
of implementation – some have
already been commissioned,
several are under construction
and others are under the bidding
process. The planned transmission
schemes would be taken up
progressively for implementation
commensurate with the RE
capacity addition.
The transmission plan for
renewable energy is a major
step towards achievement of the

planned under ISTS and Intra-state

for integration of additional

ckm and 8,27,600 MVA,

` 7,93,300 Crores.
Increasing solar power capacity11
India’s solar journey is powering
its emergence as a global clean
energy leader. The surge in
solar installations over the past
decade has played a pivotal role
in doubling India’s total installed
electricity capacity. Presently, solar
capacity stands at 150.30 GW,
while the non-fossil electricity
capacity has crossed 283.50 GW,
accounting for over 50% of the
nation’s total installed power
capacity as of March 2026,
marking a historic shift toward
low-carbon energy. By coupling
rapid domestic deployment with
global collaboration, India is laying
the foundation for a resilient,
sustainable and solar-powered
energy future that sets benchmarks
for the world.
Wind power capacity
India is now the 3rd largest wind
manufacturing hub, set to meet
10% of global demand and create
~1,54,000 new jobs (Scaling
annual installations to 15 GW).
As per the Global Wind Energy
Council (GWEC), today unveiled
its latest report “Wind at the Core:
Driving India’s Green Ambitions
and International Influence, India’s
installed wind capacity can more
than double from 54.60 GW to
107 GW by 2030, in line with
state-level Resource Adequacy
Plans (RAP). This is pivotal to help
India achieve least cost pathway
for a successful energy transition.
Addressing grid concerns,
strengthening RPO compliance
and aligning bidding processes

with state offtake needs could
push installations toward the
full potential.
An evolving regulatory
environment
India stands at a pivotal point
in its energy transition. India's
energy security is a critical
component of its economic
growth and sustainability
goals. The government has
launched various schemes
aimed at promoting renewable
energy, enhancing grid
stability, and reducing
carbon emissions.
India’s ambition to establish itself
as a renewable energy leader is
driven by a set of stable, conducive
industrial policies. Solar and wind
energy have been the mainstay
of India's energy transition, with
installed solar capacity increasing
39-fold since 2014 and wind
capacity doubling. Government
schemes for solar energy in India,
such as the PM Surya Ghar Muft
Bijli Yojana, PM-KUSUM, the Solar
Parks Scheme and the National
Wind-Solar Hybrid Policy, are
expanding access and accelerating
deployment across states. These
key initiatives reflect the nation's
commitment to a cleaner and
self-reliant energy future.

to reach 459 GW, while total

The success of this transition
is contingent on a stable
investment climate and robust
implementation mechanisms.
While significant strides have
been made in renewable energy
adoption, addressing regulatory
inconsistencies and financial
instability will be critical in
sustaining this momentum. India’s
power sector transformation
requires a multi-pronged approach.
Strengthening discom finances,
ensuring regulatory certainty and
expanding grid infrastructure will be
key to unlocking the full potential
of clean energy investments.

Incentives for capacity addition
with Atmanirbhar Bharat
Through bold policy reforms,
targeted investment incentives
and rapid scale-up of domestic
manufacturing, India is now laying
the foundation for an Atmanirbhar
Urja sector — one where every
solar module, wind turbine,
bio-pellet and green hydrogen
molecule increasingly comes from
Indian soil, fuels Indian industry and
powers Indian homes.
In the last five years, the
government has implemented
following schemes with an aim
to establish and facilitate electric
mobility in the country to reduce
vehicular emission and dependence
on fossil fuels:

– Faster Adoption and
Manufacturing of (Hybrid
&) Electric Vehicles in India
(FAME India) Scheme.

– Production Linked Incentive
(PLI) Scheme for Automobile
and Auto Component Industry in
India (PLI-Auto).

– Production Linked Incentive
Scheme on National Programme
on Advanced Chemistry Cell
(ACC) Battery Storage.

– PM Electric Drive Revolution in
Innovative Vehicle Enhancement
(PM E-DRIVE) Scheme.

– PM e-Bus Sewa-Payment
Security Mechanism
(PSM) Scheme.

– Scheme for Promotion of
Manufacturing of Electric
Passenger Cars in India
(SPMEPCI).

C O R P O R AT E OV E RV I E W

Green hydrogen
India's net-zero ambitions extend
beyond just adopting renewables.
They would be incomplete
without the country becoming a
global center for the production,
utilization and export of green
hydrogen. To realize this vision,
the government launched the
National Green Hydrogen Mission
(NGHM) that aims to build the
capacity and ecosystem required
to position India as a global leader
in clean hydrogen. March 2026
The Mission focuses on four
key pillars, including policy and
regulatory framework, demand
creation, research and
development & innovation
and enabling infrastructure and
ecosystem development — aimed
at positioning India as a global hub
for green hydrogen production, use
and export.
Launched in January 2023,
the NGHM has an initial outlay of
` 19,744 Crores till FY 2029-30,
which includes ` 17,490 Crores for
the Strategic Interventions
for Green Hydrogen
Transition (SIGHT) program,
` 1,466 Crores for pilot projects,
` 400 Crores for research and
development (R&D) and
` 388 Crores towards other
mission components. The Mission
is expected to create over 6 Lakh
jobs, reduce fossil fuel imports by
more than ` 1 Lakh Crore and avoid
nearly 50 MMT of greenhouse gas
emissions every year by 2030.

23

PLI schemes – a key pillar
to position India as a global
manufacturing hub

dependence in the area of
renewable energy.

India's potential for global
leadership in renewable energy
is rooted in its ability to execute
projects at a massive scale and
achieve some of the world's
lowest costs for solar and wind
energy. The country has leveraged
its competitive landscape and
innovative tender design to achieve
low tariffs for solar plus hybrid
battery, reaching as low as
` 2.70 kilowatt hour, making clean
energy an economically rational
choice for new power generation.

Further, the National Programme
on Advanced Chemistry Cell (ACC)
Battery Storage is a key initiative by
the government to boost domestic
manufacturing of advanced battery
cells for electric mobility. The
program aims to establish 50 GWh
of domestic ACC manufacturing
capacity, reducing reliance on
imports and strengthening the
electric vehicle ecosystem.

As global supply chains undergo
their deepest realignment in
decades, the Production Linked
Incentive (PLI) scheme has
emerged as a defining framework
of industrial renewal. With an
outlay of nearly ` 1.91 Lakh Crore,
it rewards performance rather
than promises, anchoring India’s
transition from policy aspiration to
industrial execution.
The Ministry of Renewable
Energy (MNRE) is implementing
the production-linked incentive
scheme for High Efficiency Solar
PV modules with a total outlay
of ` 24,000 Crores to enhance
domestic manufacturing and to
reduce import dependency. Solar
PV manufacturers are selected
through a transparent selection
process. The scheme aims to build
an ecosystem for manufacturing of
high efficiency solar PV modules
in India and thus reduce import

Future outlook
India's renewable energy journey
is no longer just a national effort.
It has become a global blueprint
for climate action and economic
growth. The convergence
of ambitious targets, strong
policy frameworks, unwavering
international confidence, progress
in green hydrogen and the
vital ability to deliver scale and
manufacturing makes India a
distinctive powerhouse.
As technology costs decrease
and global capital increasingly
seeks climate-compatible
investments, India emerges as
a model for balancing growth
with responsibility. Over the
next decade, India will not only
meet its own targets, but also
export its expertise, technology
and clean energy solutions
worldwide. By leading this
transformation, India demonstrates
that the path to a net-zero
future can be both inclusive and
economically transformative.

The Grid Automation team of Hitachi Energy India

Hitachi Energy India Limited

The COMFLEX testing process was automated
using a PLC-based system with a Human Machine
Interface (HMI), making the process faster, easier
and more accurate. Productivity increased by over
20%, while the testing time was reduced from
15 minutes to less than 10 minutes. The system
also records data in real time and automatically
generates test reports. A single universal test fixture
can be used for all COMFLEX variants.

PGTR bushing team awarded gold at
QCFI convention 2025

The Transformer Bushing team was honored with
the Gold award at the QCFI (Quality Circle Forum of
India) Convention 2025. The case study on Kaizen
initiatives for Muda reduction and productivity
improvement for Dry Bushings with the concept of
“Feedback to Fulfilment” earned top recognition
and showcased the team's commitment to
continuous improvement.

Hitachi Energy's power transformer factory
secures NABL re-accreditation

Successfully completed National Accreditation
Board for Testing and Calibration
Laboratories(NABL) – ISO/IEC 17025:2017
re-accreditation audit, a strong testament to the
team's unwavering commitment to quality and
technical excellence, driven by the Company's
focus on sustainable and continuous improvement.
This was a result of the robust process controls,
which ensured accuracy and reliability of the testing
procedures. The Company prioritized eco-conscious
practices, aligning with its long-term sustainability
goals, fostering strong team collaboration and
knowledge-sharing.

Received special recognition under the
Emerging Sustainable Practices

The Company’s Doddaballapur facility received
special recognition at CII Karnataka ESG Summit
2025 under the Emerging Sustainable Practices
category, highlighting its commitment to
sustainable manufacturing excellence. The Plant
also received the Bronze award at the CII National
EHS Excellence Awards 2025 in the Energy and
Utilities category.

Received platinum-level certification for
zero waste to landfill

The Halol manufacturing facility was certified
as “Water Positive”, following an independent
reasonable-level verification of the Water
Balance Index for the fiscal year FY 2025-26.
The Halol and Mysuru facilities also received
“Platinum Level” certification for Zero Waste
to Landfill, as per independent verification
conducted by SGS India Pvt. Ltd. This marks
a significant milestone in the Company’s
sustainability journey and strongly reinforces its
commitment towards environmental stewardship
across operations.

Stakeholder
engagement

C9

Shareholders/
Investors

Needs and expectations

Hitachi Energy India Limited
has identified its key
stakeholders and channels
of communication with
them, being aligned with the
activities of its businesses.
It regularly engages with
all its stakeholders with the
aim of understanding their
challenges, expectations
and issues in order to
generate and maximize the
value provided.

– Sustainable long-term returns backed by strong
financial performance.

– Regular dividends and increasing profitability.
– Proactive and effective risk management practices.
– Adoption of best-in-class corporate governance standards.
– Transparent, timely and accurate statutory disclosures,
including compliance related filings.

Risk assessment and management
Effective risk management is essential to sustain growth and achieve the
Company’s strategic objectives. Your Company proactively identifies and
manages risks through an integrated approach embedded in strategic planning,
business targets and daily operations.
The ability to create long-term value depends on how effectively the risks are mitigated and opportunities
are leveraged. Key risks have been identified and appropriate mitigation strategies have been implemented
based on their potential impact. Mitigation strategies have been developed and applied in line with the
assessed impact of each risk.

Governance with integrity
and accountability

Continuing the essence of good
governance
During Financial Year 2025–26,
the Company continued to
uphold a robust and progressive
corporate governance framework
anchored in integrity, transparency,
accountability, fairness and equity.
At Hitachi Energy India Limited,
governance is not viewed merely
as a mechanism for regulatory
compliance, but as a foundation
for ethical conduct, responsible
leadership, effective risk oversight
and sustainable long-term
value creation.
The Company seeks to safeguard
stakeholder interests and
strengthen confidence through
transparent decision-making,

Integrated Annual Report 2025-26

responsible business conduct
and clearly defined governance
practices. The Board of Directors,
supported by its committees and
senior leadership, provides strategic
direction and balanced judgment.
Governance structure
The Company’s governance
structure is designed to ensure
effective oversight, informed
decision-making and clear
accountability at all levels. The
Board of Directors, together with
its duly constituted Committees,
plays a pivotal role in guiding the
Company’s strategic direction
and overseeing compliance with
applicable regulatory requirements
and governance practices. This
structure supports transparency,

informed decision-making and
a culture of integrity and ethical
conduct across the organization.
Responsible leadership
Your Company is built on the
promising leadership pillars that
provide purposeful direction,
fostering connections among
individuals to promote their
potential, enhance performance
and achieve purpose-driven
growth. The Board comprises
distinguished professionals
with diverse expertise,
experience and integrity, who
bring independent judgment
and balanced perspectives to
Board’s deliberations.

44

C O R P O R AT E OV E RV I E W

The Directors actively participate
in all the meetings and strategic
discussions, contributing to
sustainable growth and resilience
of the Company. Their collective
wisdom, strategic guidance
and an unwavering dedication
to excellence are instrumental
in shaping your Company’s
trajectory as a leader in delivering
innovative, reliable and sustainable
energy solutions. This robust
leadership framework not only
drives its operational success, it
also reinforces the commitment
to creating value for all the
stakeholders. During the year,
the Independent Directors held
exclusive meetings, including
independent sessions with the
Statutory Auditors, without the
presence of the Management.
Corporate governance framework
The Company’s corporate
governance framework is
aligned with the Code of Ethics
and Business Conduct and
supports delivery of strategic
objectives through ethical
conduct, transparency and
accountability at every level.
The framework is reinforced
through robust anti-bribery and
anti-corruption controls, conflict
of interest disclosures, gifts
and entertainment governance,
employee awareness and
channels for reporting concerns.
Your Company is also certified
for ISO 37001:2016 for its
anti-bribery management system.
Internal control framework
The Company’s internal control
framework is designed to safeguard
the interests of all its stakeholders
by fostering an environment
conducive to effective operations.
It addresses both financial and
operational risks through a
comprehensive structure that
includes a country management
committee, group directives and
instructions, local management

45

instructions, entity-level controls,
process level controls and
management testing programs.
This comprehensive approach
ensures that the control processes
are proportionate to the size and
complexity of the Company.
Internal audit
Your Company has a
well-established, independent,
multi-disciplinary internal
audit team which operates
in accordance with the best
practices of governance. The
internal audit function operates
independently and adopts a
risk-based approach to evaluate
the adequacy and effectiveness
of internal controls, governance
processes and risk management
systems. The internal audit team
provides valuable insights into
the management and the Audit
Committee, thereby supporting
continuous improvement and
operational excellence.
Compliance and regulatory
assurance
Compliance is a vital foundation
of the Company’s operations
and governance framework,
ensuring that all the relevant
laws and industry standards
are met. The commitment to
integrity and accountability is
reflected in the comprehensive
compliance framework, supported
by the “Legatrix software”,
which automates the mapping
of applicable laws and assigns
responsibilities to identified internal
owners. Quarterly compliance
certificates are obtained from
relevant departments and functions
and placed before the Board on a
quarterly basis. During
FY 2025–26, the Company
maintained strong compliance
standards and no material audit
observations were reported by the
Secretarial Auditors or the Statutory
Auditors in relation to governance
practices or statutory compliances.

Ethics and integrity
The Company’s Code of Ethics
and Business Conduct provide
clear guidance to the Directors,
employees and relevant
stakeholders on expected
standards of ethical behavior,
conflict of interest, integrity and
accountability. Your Company has
implemented a structured approach
to prevent, detect, review and
address any potential integrity
concerns. This is supported by
tools, processes and reporting
channels and a zero-tolerance
policy for violations. The policy
is available at https://www.
hitachienergy.com/in/en/investorrelations/board-of-directors.
Whistle-blower policy / vigil
mechanism
The Company has an effective
whistle-blower policy and vigil
mechanism that enables Directors,
employees and third parties
to report concerns relating to
unethical or inappropriate behavior
or suspected fraud or violation of
the Company’s Code of Conduct or
leak of unpublished price-sensitive
information and related matters.
Adequate safe-guards are provided
to protect whistle-blowers against
retaliation and reported matters are
addressed in a fair, confidential and
timely manner. The whistle-blower
policy of the Company is available
at https://www.hitachienergy.
com/in/en/company/our-peopleand-culture/integrity/reportingchannels/whistleblower-protectionpolicy.

Capex for capacity increase

Ongoing capital expansion

In October 2024, the Company
announced a capex plan of ` 2,000
Crores. Later, an additional capex
of ` 2,000 Crores was planned
to be initiated and implemented.
In addition to other projects, a
greenfield large power transformer
facility is planned to be set up at
Karjan at Vadodara, Gujarat. This
has taken the cumulative capex to
` 4,000 Crores announced.

The Company continues to invest
in strategic capacity expansion
to support the growing demand
and strengthen its manufacturing
and innovation capabilities. With
expansion in Mysuru, Hitachi
Energy India Limited is proud to
support India’s growing grid and
renewable energy integration, while
strengthening the local energy
manufacturing ecosystem for a
sustainable future.
The expansion of its Mysuru
facility enhances the local supply
of transformer-grade pressboard,
while establishing an ultralow carbon production line,
reinforcing supply chain resilience

53

and supporting renewable
energy integration. As part of
the expansion, will replace the
fossil fuel boiler, making its
Mysuru site an ultra-low carbon
pressboard facility.
Additionally, the Company is
expanding its Global Technology
and Innovation Centre in Chennai,
further advancing its commitment
to innovation and sustainable
growth. The comprehensive
Service portfolio of the Company
supports customers at every stage
of their asset lifecycle – from
planning through installation
and commissioning to life-time
extension and sustainable end-oflife solutions.

and hiring approach, including
internship and graduate

parameters. Placement officers
received inputs on improving
student-readiness through strong

and a learning mindset, along with

Mega Recruitment drive:
Hitachi Energy conducted
a Mega Recruitment Drive

to attract and onboard highpotential early-career talent across
key functions. The drive brought
together eligible candidates through

designed to assess technical

providing candidates with an overview
of Hitachi Energy’s purpose, business
focus and its contribution to building

energy system.

reinforced Hitachi Energy’s
commitment to strengthening its
talent pipeline through inclusive

engagement, creating opportunities
for graduates to begin impactful
careers with learning, mentorship and
growth pathways.

ConnectHER:
ConnectHER, an exclusive
career fair and networking event
by Hitachi Energy was held in
Bangalore. Celebrating the power
of connection and opportunity
for women in energy, the event
brought together 170 highly
relevant, shortlisted women
candidates across both in-person

was marked by energy, meaningful

inclusive careers in the industry.
Launch of Menstrual leave policy:
Hitachi Energy India announced
the launch of its Menstrual Leave
Policy as a progressive step toward
strengthening employee wellbeing,
health and safety, while advancing
an inclusive workplace culture. The
policy recognizes that menstrual
health is a normal and important
aspect of wellbeing and reinforces
the organization’s commitment
to supporting employees through
practical, empathetic benefits.
Under the policy, all women
employees are eligible for one
working day of paid menstrual
leave per month (up to 12 days
per calendar year), in addition to
existing leave entitlements.

Social capital
CSR policy of the Company
Hitachi Energy India Limited (“the
Company”) as a responsible
corporate entity strives to reach
out to the wider community to
ensure the well-being of the
needy as a part of its Corporate
Social Responsibility (“CSR”). The
Company has always believed in
and contributed to society.
Along with sustained economic
performance, environmental and
social stewardship is also a key
factor for holistic business growth
and is internalized as an integral
part of the Company’s strategy.
The Company views corporate
social responsibility as a channel
to further its cause of “bringing
power to all by shaping the future
of sustainable energy”.
The Company is committed
to sustainable and inclusive
development of the community’s
social capital through active
engagement. CSR serves as a
strategic channel that helps shape
the future of sustainable energy
by fostering the sustainable and
inclusive development of social
capital. Through its CSR initiatives,
your Company strengthens
communities, builds resilience
and supports long-term progress
aligned with broader environmental
and social goals.
The CSR policy outlines its core
aims and objectives, focusing
on creating meaningful impact
through responsible engagement,
community development and
stakeholder collaboration.
Embedded within the overall
corporate strategy, CSR is an
integral driver of sustainable
growth, which enables your
Company to align business success
with social value creation.
At Hitachi Energy India Limited, a
structured approach to corporate
responsibility is reflected in the

63

formulation of a dedicated CSR
policy. The Company has also
identified key focus areas to
guide its CSR initiatives, ensuring
that its efforts are aligned
with community needs, while
contributing to sustainable and
inclusive development.

Key CSR initiatives in
FY 2025-26
Women In Engineering
Women in Engineering is a
multiyear program for 250 girl
students from underprivileged
families and hailing from tier 2/3
towns, pursuing their UG course in
core engineering are adapted under
this program. The students are
spread across seven states and one
union territory, Karnataka, Tamil
Nadu, Gujarat, Delhi, Haryana,
Telangana, Andhra Pradesh and
Maharashtra. During the year, these
students are provided with over 25
trainings on soft skills, technical,
mentoring for career planning
and preparing for employability.
Over 12 women employees
actively engaged in conducting
the trainings. The students were
also provided annual scholarship,
industry visits, sponsored resident
training programs, several students
received internship at Company
business locations and potential
employment opportunities.

benefited over 1000 students.

and provided 36kW of solar

students at Vadodara Municipal
Corporation schools.

Emissions management

Rooftop solar

1,631 kWp
– Electricity sourced through PPA.
– International renewable energy
certificates (IREC) purchased.

– Advanced decarbonization
efforts by adopting low-carbon
energy solutions, including
a biofuel boiler at Mysuru,
delivering ~2,700 tCO₂e annual
emissions reduction.

– Strengthened decarbonization
through the adoption of
battery-operated/EV forklifts at
Maneja and Mysuru, avoiding
approximately 90 tCO₂e
emissions annually.

– Progressed the transition
away from ozone-depleting
refrigerants by replacing R22
systems with R410A, including
full replacement of a 30 TR unit
in TR Admin and partial (50% /
15 TR) capacity upgrade in the
TR 2nd Floor LTB office.

– Enhanced energy efficiency by
replacing an electrical pump with
a pneumatic pump at the diesel
tank, avoiding approximately
0.65 tCO₂e emissions annually.

– Expanded renewable energy
footprint with installed solar
capacity of 931 kWp at Maneja,
600 kWp at Doddaballapur and
100 kW at Khavda.

– Enhanced air quality
management by upgrading
the exhaust duct and cleaning
the fan in the painting booth,
improving suction efficiency
and minimizing paint dust/
particulate emissions.
Energy management

– Installed motion sensors in
washrooms and meeting rooms,
resulted in energy savings by
controlling operation of lights
during day time.

Integrated Annual Report 2025-26

– Replaced conventional lights
with LED lights, saving 39,000
kWh/annum.

– Enabled stabilized voltage
in lighting circuits in dry and
traction transformer factory by
installing lighting transformer
in plant and office area light
fixtures, also saving energy.

– Installed solar tubes on factory
roof, resulting in improving
lux level in factory area and
energy savings.
Water stewardship

– Enhanced water management
through installation of smart
water meters with digital
monitoring at Maneja,
enabling real-time visibility of
consumption patterns.

– Strengthened water efficiency
by installing 23 water meters
across the distribution network,
enabling unit-level consumption
tracking, leak detection and
optimization; resulting in a
reduction of ~132 KL/day in
overall water consumption.

– Promoted water reuse through
deployment of multiple reuse
systems, including:
– Utilization of STP-treated
water for irrigation and
cleaning purposes.
– Reuse of RO reject water for
canteen operations, flushing
and utilities.
– Achieved fresh water savings
of ~14 KL/day through reuse
of RO reject water in canteen
operations, including utensil
washing and floor cleaning in
Maneja facility.
– Improved water efficiency by
adopting low-flow fixtures
across office facilities,
reducing overall water
demand.

including installation of
3 recharge structures,
contributing to groundwater
replenishment.
– Achieved Water Positive
Index certification at Halol,
supported by rainwater
recharge and water
conservation measures.
Waste management

– Strengthened circular economy
practices by replacing wooden
packaging with corrugated
boxes, delivering ~103 tCO2e
annual emissions savings and
reducing packaging consumption
by ~2,400 boxes per year.

– Enhanced material efficiency
through reuse of wooden
boxes, conserving ~20 MT of
wood annually.

– Achieved Zero Waste to Landfill/
Incineration at Maneja by
diverting 110 MT of hazardous
waste for co-processing,
ensuring environmentally
responsible disposal.

– Attained Zero Waste to Landfill
Platinum certification from

Halol, reinforcing commitment
to sustainable waste
management practices.

– Reduced hazardous waste
generation by optimizing
the use of Shell Diala
flushing oil, minimizing
chemical consumption and
waste generation.

– Promoted material circularity
through reuse of wooden pallets
and boxes, reducing dependence
on virgin wood resources and
minimizing waste.

– Advanced plastic reduction
initiatives by replacing
single-use plastic water bottles
with reusable glass bottles
across facilities.

sustainability by

A brief on types of customers

		The Company serves a diverse customer base across utilities, industrial sectors, transportation, data centers,
and infrastructure. Its customers include Engineering, Procurement & Construction (EPC) contractors,
distributors, Original Equipment Manufacturers (OEMs), and channel partners operating within these
segments. Through its five business units, Transformers, High Voltage, Grid Integration, Grid Automation,
and Service, the Company provides integrated and digital solutions, including software, protection and
control systems, substation integration (220 kV to 765 kV), power and traction transformers, capacitors
and filters, as well as advanced technologies such as SF₆-free GIS under the EconiQ portfolio.

A global sustainability material impact assessment, applicable to all regions, was undertaken to identify and prioritize
the sustainability impacts that matter most to our stakeholders across the value chain. The material impacts included
in the assessment are relevant to the Company globally. The impacts highlighted in the top right corner and marked
in red as ‘critical’ are the key impacts considered substantive to the Company’s strategy, reporting, and action. Within
our enterprise risk management (ERM) framework we assess impact over the short-term (less than two years),
medium-term (two to five years) and long term (more than five years) perspective.

7. Statement by director responsible for the business responsibility report, highlighting ESG related challenges, targets and
achievements (listed entity has flexibility regarding the placement of this disclosure)
	Sustainability is fundamental to our purpose of advancing a sustainable energy future for all. Guided by robust governance
and a materiality-led approach, we integrate Environmental, Social, and Governance (ESG) considerations across our strategy,
operations, and partnerships, ensuring responsible growth and long-term stakeholder value (P1, P2).
	We continue to advance our decarbonization roadmap aligned with science-based targets, focusing on reducing emissions
and increasing renewable energy adoption. Our environmental performance is strengthened through initiatives such as Water
Positive certification at our Halol facility, Platinum-level Zero Waste to Landfill certification at Halol and Mysore, and ongoing
efforts to reduce biodiversity impacts (P3, P6).
	As a technology leader, we contribute to India’s energy transition through advanced solutions including HVDC and digital
grid technologies, supporting renewable integration, grid resilience, and access to reliable, low-carbon energy (P2, P8). Our
people remain central to our success. We prioritize health and safety, foster diversity, equity, and inclusion, and uphold human
rights across our operations (P3, P5).
	At the same time, we engage responsibly with customers, partners, and communities, ensuring transparency, trust, and value
creation (P4, P9).
	Strong governance underpins our journey. The Board provides oversight on ESG strategy, risk management, and compliance,
reinforcing a culture of integrity, ethical conduct, and accountability across the value chain (P1, P7).
	As we move forward, we remain committed to strengthening ESG performance and contributing meaningfully to a sustainable
and inclusive future. We are serious in our commitment to drive our business in a sustainable way. We have been consistent in
our efforts to decarbonize our operations and in ensuring welfare of our stakeholders. We have made considerable progress
towards achieving our Sustainability 2030 goals in all areas.

SECTION C: PRINCIPLE WISE PERFORMANCE DISCLOSURE
This section is aimed at helping entities demonstrate their performance in integrating the Principles and Core Elements
with key processes and decisions. The information sought is categorized as “Essential” and “Leadership”. While the
essential indicators are expected to be disclosed by every entity that is mandated to file this report, the leadership
indicators may be voluntarily disclosed by entities which aspire to progress to a higher level in their quest to be
socially, environmentally and ethically responsible.
PRINCIPLE 1 BUSINESSES SHOULD CONDUCT AND GOVERN THEMSELVES WITH INTEGRITY, AND IN A
MANNER THAT IS ETHICAL, TRANSPARENT AND ACCOUNTABLE.
Integrity, transparency, and accountability
The Company’s Code of Ethics and Business Conduct consists of rules and principles to assist leaders and employees
in making decisions and acting ethically and in line with the Company’s Identity. It applies to anyone who works for or
represents the Company, including members of the board of directors, management, employees and contractors of
the Company. Company expects all suppliers to adhere to similar standards and to conduct their business ethically,
as stated in the Company’s Supplier Code of Conduct. The Company has developed and implemented a systematic
approach designed to prevent, detect and resolve any potential integrity concerns. This is supported by tools and
processes, robust Speak-up Channels and a zero-tolerance policy for any violations or unethical business behavior.

Leadership Indicators
1.

Awareness programmes conducted for value chain partners on any of the Principles during the financial year:

	In India, supplier trainings on all 9 BRSR core principles were conducted twice in FY2025-26. Training material
was made available in the Company’s supplier portal for all suppliers. For suppliers with higher sustainability
risks, we offer a Supplier Sustainability Development Program (SSDP) that prioritizes partners according to a
risk matrix, aggregating country and commodity risks, operational characteristics, the supplier’s criticality, and
spending level. This program includes training, awareness, and capacity-building. In the year 2025-26, as a part
of supplier sustainability development program, the Company trained 10 suppliers within India. The Supplier
Sustainability Development Program (SSDP) is a supplier engagement to uphold human rights and decent
working conditions, ensure safe and environmentally sound operations and comply with relevant regulatory
requirements in the supply chain

2

(a) Does the entity have procedures in place for sustainable sourcing? (Yes/No)

		Yes
2.

b)

If yes, what percentage of inputs were sourced sustainably?

		Considering the nature of our business, a significant proportion of our purchased goods comprises metals
and metal-based semi-finished products, which are non-renewable materials. Accordingly, the percentage
of sustainably sourced inputs is limited to ~10%. However, sustainability considerations are integrated into
our procurement processes through supplier assessments, responsible sourcing practices, and adherence
to applicable environmental and social standards.
3.

Describe the processes in place to safely reclaim your products for reusing, recycling and disposing at the end
of life, for (a) Plastics (including packaging) (b) E-waste (c) Hazardous waste and (d) other waste.

	The Company adopts a circular economy approach across the lifecycle of its products, with a focus on minimizing
resource consumption, reducing waste generation, and ensuring safe end-of-life management. Materials such
as steel, copper, aluminum, plastics, and insulating oil used in the Company’s products are largely recoverable
and recyclable.
The processes in place for safe reclamation, recycling, reuse, and disposal are outlined below:
Plastics (including packaging)

•

Plastic packaging waste is segregated at source and sent to authorized recyclers for processing. The
Company complies with applicable Extended Producer Responsibility (EPR) requirements for plastic
packaging waste. Efforts are ongoing to increase the use of recyclable and sustainable packaging materials
and reduce plastic consumption. During the reporting period, 64.94 MT of plastic waste was recycled.

E-waste

•

Electrical and electronic components are designed to facilitate safe dismantling and material recovery at
end-of-life. Customers are provided guidance on environmentally sound disposal of e-waste through product
manuals. E-waste is channelized through authorized recyclers to maximize material recovery and prevent
environmental contamination. The Company complies with applicable Extended Producer Responsibility
(EPR) requirements for E-waste. During the reporting period 16.63 MT of e-waste was recycled.

Hazardous waste

•

Hazardous waste is managed in accordance with applicable environmental, health and safety regulatory
and other requirements. Processes are in place across our facilities for collection, storage, transportation,
recycling, treatment and disposal through authorized vendors. Product documentation includes instructions
for safe handling and disposal of hazardous components at end-of-life. During the reporting period, 462.30
MT of hazardous waste was recycled and 79.32 MT was safely disposed through authorized treatment
and disposal facilities. The Company has successfully diverted waste from landfill through robust waste
segregation, recycling, recovery, and authorized disposal practices.

Other waste

•

The Company has recycled other wastes such as ferrous and non ferrous metal scrap, wood and paper
wastes through authorized recycling partners. Waste management practices are aligned with resource
efficiency, waste minimization, and regulatory compliance principles. During the reporting period, 8,329
MT of non-hazardous waste was recycled.

	Overall, the Company integrates design for environment, regulatory compliance, and stakeholder awareness
into its end-of-life product management strategy, ensuring safe disposal practices while maximizing resource
recovery and minimizing environmental impact.

 hether Extended Producer Responsibility (EPR) is applicable to the entity’s activities (Yes / No). If yes,

whether the waste collection plan is in line with the Extended Producer Responsibility (EPR) plan submitted

Yes, Extended Producer Responsibility (EPR) is applicable to the Company’s operations.

	The Company has established a waste collection and management system that is fully aligned with the EPR
plan submitted to the Central Pollution Control Board (CPCB) and relevant regulatory authorities. The waste
collection plan is implemented in accordance with applicable rules and guidelines to ensure environmentally
sound management of waste arising from its products and packaging.
The Company ensures that:

•

The Company has established a waste collection and management system that is fully aligned with the EPR

Leadership Indicators
1.

 as the entity conducted Life Cycle Perspective / Assessments (LCA) for any of its products (for manufacturing
H
industry) or for its services (for service industry)? If yes, provide details in the following format?

	The Company’s lifecycle perspective on managing environmental risks, aspects, and impacts extends beyond its
operations. It includes its supply chain, the use of its products, and their end-of-life. The application of lifecycle
assessments (LCAs) is key to improving the environmental performance of our products, systems, and services.
LCAs provide us with quantitative environmental information to improve the design of our products, systems,
and services. Beyond that, LCAs quantify the environmental footprint of our products, systems, and services as
requested by our customers.
	LCAs are performed as per ISO 14040 and ISO 14044 on request. For our main product groups, we have
assessed lifecycle environmental impact and provided transparent environmental information to our customers
and regulators. Where needed, we verify LCAs according to ISO 14025 to provide an Environmental Product
Declaration (EPD) based on LCAs. The Company understands the complexity of its portfolio and that systems
it delivers are customized to its customer’s needs and specifications.
	Over previous years, however, the Company has also performed LCAs for its solutions of complex systems
such as High Voltage Direct Current (HVDC), Flexible Alternating Current Transmission Systems (FACTS), grid
connections, e-mobility solutions, and others. The LCAs helped identify the next focus areas for eco-design
and improvements. The Company is investigating the quantification of the environmental impact and potential
benefits of its service solutions and project installations. It is also implementing highly automated LCA tools
that directly assess project-specific configurations, providing decision support at all project stages and during
discussions with its customers.
	EcoSmartTM is the Company’s in-house lifecycle assessment calculator for transformers, components, and
services. This tool is based on lifecycle modeling by ISO 14040 and ISO 14044 standards on environmental
lifecycle assessment and the applicable product category rules (PCR) of the International EPD System. It
evaluates energy and resource consumption and environmental emissions from all lifecycle stages of transformer
solutions (production and transportation of materials and parts to manufacturing sites; unit manufacturing;
assembly and testing; transportation to customer site; product use, maintenance, and end-of-life).

	Through its Service and Digital offerings, the Company is convinced that more trusted lifecycle partnerships
will drive increased asset sustainability, given that service activities are designed to improve the sustainability
footprint of installed assets, ensure efficient and safer operation, and extend asset life. To enable circularity, the
Company has introduced eco-design for all new products, following the IEC 62430 approach.

 ercentage of recycled or reused input material to total material (by value) used in production (for manufacturing
P
industry) or providing services (for service industry).

	The Company operates in the manufacturing of transformers, which are critical capital assets with stringent
performance, safety, and reliability requirements. Accordingly, the raw materials used, primarily metals
and insulating components, are required to be of prime grade and compliant with specified technical and
quality standards.
	As a result, the use of recycled or reused input materials is limited, as it may impact product quality and
guaranteed performance parameters.
However, the Company is committed to resource efficiency and waste minimization through the following measures:

•

Optimization of manufacturing processes to ensure minimal wastage of raw materials

•

Adoption of efficient material handling and production practices

•

Recovery and reuse of SF₆ gas from products during servicing and prior to end-of-life disposal

•

 ontinuous evaluation of opportunities for incorporating sustainable materials without compromising
C
product integrity

	Given the above considerations, the percentage of recycled input material is currently not significant; however, the
Company continues to explore feasible avenues to enhance circularity while maintaining product performance
and compliance requirements.

structured focus on:

	The Company is committed to continuous learning, capability building, and strengthening health and safety

Health & Safety Training
	All employees and workers undergo mandatory training on health and safety (HSE) measures, including

10. Health and safety management system:
a.

Whether an occupational health and safety management system has been implemented by the entity?
(Yes/ No). If yes, the coverage such system?

		Yes, all our 8 manufacturing sites have implemented and are certified for Occupational Health and Safety
management system as per ISO 45001:2018.
b.

 hat are the processes used to identify work-related hazards and assess risks on a routine and nonW
routine basis by the entity?

		The Company has established a comprehensive and systematic framework to identify work-related hazards
and assess risks across both routine and non-routine operations.
		The Company identifies and evaluates Health, Safety, and Environmental (HSE) risks and opportunities at
multiple levels, ranging from individual tasks to enterprise-level activities, ensuring comprehensive coverage
across operations.
		

A specialized and standardized audit program is implemented to:

		

o

Verify the accuracy and effectiveness of risk assessments

		

o

Evaluate the efficiency of control measures

		

o

Identify opportunities for continuous improvement

		Occupational health, safety and environmental risks are assessed in line with globally recognized standards,
including ISO 14001:2015 (Environmental Management) and ISO 45001:2018 (Occupational Health &
Safety)

95

© 2026 Hitachi Energy. All rights reserved.

		Identified hazards, environmental aspects, and improvement opportunities are recorded and tracked
through a company-wide web-based tool, enabling real-time reporting and monitoring, centralized data
management and improved transparency and accountability
		Activities involving high-risk hazards, whether routine or non-routine, are subject to detailed analysis
and enhanced control measures. Based on risk assessments, additional safeguards and mitigations are
implemented, preventive and corrective actions are defined and tracked and focus is placed on eliminating
or minimizing risks at source
		The framework extends to all activities carried out by or on behalf of the Company, ensuring that contractors
comply with established HSE requirements.
c.

 hether you have processes for workers to report the work-related hazards and to remove themselves
W
from such risks. (Y/N)

		Yes. The Company promotes a strong safety culture with an open and transparent reporting environment
to ensure that health, safety, and environment considerations are prioritized in all decisions. All employees,
contractors, and stakeholders are required and encouraged to report incidents, unsafe conditions, and near
misses to their line managers, with details recorded in the company-wide web-based tool for tracking and
analysis. HSE performance data is updated regularly and made accessible through Power BI dashboards,
enabling effective monitoring of trends and performance.
		The Company has established structured tools and practices to empower workers to proactively identify
and mitigate risks. The ‘Stop! Take 5’ tool encourages individuals to pause, assess risks, and take preventive
actions before commencing work. In addition, the Time Out for Safety (TOFS) process enables employees,
contractors, and visitors to immediately stop work if unsafe conditions, behaviors, or hazards are identified.
		The Company ensures that all individuals have both the right and obligation to stop work without fear of
reprisal if they perceive a risk. Work is only resumed once the identified risks are adequately mitigated,
reinforcing a culture of shared responsibility, accountability, and continuous improvement in workplace safety.
d.

		Yes. Employees and workers of the Company have access to comprehensive non-occupational medical
and healthcare services supporting their overall well-being.

		The Company promotes holistic well-being through its global “Mind Matters” initiative, delivered via the

access personalized wellness programs and courses, use guided meditation tools and receive professional
counselling support. The app ensures confidentiality and participation is voluntary. It is available in 25+
languages, with counselling support in 40+ languages. Resilience training programs for employees and
various mental health awareness campaigns, including global observances and resource promotion are part
of mental health initiatives.
